The theological identifications are structural isomorphisms at cataphatic grade, religious exclusivity disclaimed, load-bearing on nothing in any verdict. The Kalam's divine-kind predication is a statement about the Kalam of Allah ﷺ in its residence and does not transfer to the vessel: Q 5:75, kānā yaʾkulāni aṭ-ṭaʿām, they both used to eat food, and Q 19:30-33 hold the vessel at the creaturely floor. The universality of min rūḥī forecloses any argument from the surviving layer to a unique rank, since a proof of exemption from it would prove the same for Adam AS and for every human. The shubbiha lahum passage at Q 4:157 splits by agent and by knowledge, denying their accomplishment and their certainty on the stated ground that they had no knowledge but the following of conjecture, and is read against the nafs and wajh layers of the parallel-register master and the resident cosmogony reading, conditional on continuous-field monism and out of band. W_social equal to zero in both directions throughout: the field's it-is-old and the reading's it-is-new are both consensus and both zeroed. ΔM equal to zero, the arrangement the whole contribution, no new mathematics and no new philology claimed, M1 failing at once on the positive-mass cascade and the Mosaic Seal holding.

### SC-058 · CN-PSP-CLEAN-GOSPEL-MARK-01 · The Clean Gospel of Mark · The Scripture-Bearer Asymmetry, the Distilled Quranic Christology, and the Whole of the Earliest Gospel Read Passage by Passage from Within the Corpus

Status: SEALED-RESIDENT · Book IV · [⟀] on the textual inventories and the register-resolution · [⟀ S] on the four-verse architecture, the voluntariness guard, the two-place office, and the counterfeit law · [⟀ T] on the seven counted threads, each a concordance or manuscript fact · [⟀ LL-19] on the speech-creation convergence, sealed in direction with the residue retained · [X] on the eight corrections and on the named inflations · the identity of ʿĪsā AS at the Ground routed out of band, no token · the fence beside the faces and terminal for none · three apertures located and uncrossed · ΔM = 0
Source: sessions of 2026-08-02 and 2026-08-03, six audit rounds against two independent scribes for the first edition, then the continuous Master Edition build; published as *Quranic Christology: The Clean Gospel of Mark*, Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.21756248, PhilArchive record ISLTCG, repository copy at Publication Library/Scripture/Gospel/Mark
Depends: SC-056 the Kalima Master, whose Word-doctrine this coordinate applies and does not re-derive · SC-019 the ransom reading, reopened at exactly one clause · SC-018 and SC-020, the comparative wing this coordinate anchors · SC-013 and SC-014, whose apophatic fences govern the identity question · SC-061 and SC-062 and SC-063 and SC-064, four coordinates harvested out of this reading and seated on their own attestations
Supersedes: the first edition of this coordinate, sealed on the eighteen-section reading, which is carried forward whole and extended rather than corrected; the three findings this edition revises are named at movement XV

**I · The asymmetry, drawn in the only class that carries information.** Of the twenty-five prophets the corpus names, most appear in a list or a short cycle and carry no fault because no narrative exists in which one could be recorded; nothing follows from their silence. The class that carries information is those given a book, and there are five. Ibrāhīm AS holds the *ṣuḥuf* (Q 87:19) and confesses a *khaṭīʾa* in his own prayer (Q 26:82). Mūsā AS holds the Tawrāt and the Furqān (Q 2:53) and carries the heaviest record in the corpus. Dāwūd AS holds the Zabūr (Q 4:163) and is forgiven a named thing (Q 38:24). The Prophet ﷺ holds the Qurʾān and is corrected seven times, at Q 80:1, Q 9:43, Q 33:37, Q 8:67, Q 17:74, Q 66:1, and twice commanded to seek forgiveness at Q 40:55 and Q 47:19. ʿĪsā AS holds the Injīl (Q 5:46) and carries nothing. **Four of five, and the fifth empty.** The claim does not rest on counting minor figures and cannot be dissolved by pointing at a prophet mentioned in passing. Beside him stands Yaḥyā AS, distinctive on a different ground: the negation is stated outright, *wa-lam yakun jabbāran ʿaṣiyyā* (Q 19:14), using the root applied to Adam AS at Q 20:121 and to Hārūn AS at Q 20:93, and the corpus marks his name unprecedented at Q 19:7 and then rhymes it with living at Q 19:15.

**II · The one candidate, adjudicated rather than assumed.** A negative claim about a corpus is only as good as the search behind it, and there is exactly one passage where the Source interrogates ʿĪsā AS directly. Q 5:116 puts the accusation in question form and the answer returns clean in three moves: *subḥānak*, refusing the premise; *mā yakūnu lī an aqūla mā laysa lī bi-ḥaqq*, refusing to occupy a formulation rather than denying a deed; and the epistemic boundary, *taʿlamu mā fī nafsī wa-lā aʿlamu mā fī nafsik*, placing verification with the questioner. Then the affirmative content at Q 5:117. **An interrogation that returns nothing is evidence for the file**, and the corpus has the other outcome available and uses it freely elsewhere. Q 5:110 recounts every favour with the permission-clause four times and adds no correction; Q 43:59 gives the strongest available reduction, *in huwa illā ʿabdun anʿamnā ʿalayh*, and carries no fault with it.

**III · What the silence is for, the voluntariness guard.** The inverse relation holds across the corpus: the higher the ontological anomaly, the heavier the servanthood declaration. The first word ʿĪsā AS speaks is *ʿabd* (Q 19:30), from the cradle, before book or prophethood. Had a fault been recorded it would have become the proof of mere humanity and the servanthood would rest on deficiency. Instead Q 4:172 states it one verse after the three designations: *lan yastankifa al-Masīḥu an yakūna ʿabdan lillāh wa-lā al-malāʾikatu al-muqarrabūn*. *Yastankif* presupposes a height from which disdain would be intelligible, and the angels beside him fix that height. **Not too small to be more; not too proud to be less.**

**IV · The two-place office, and why the merger is barred.** Q 3:39 makes Yaḥyā AS *muṣaddiq* of a *kalima*, and confirmation is two-place: no one attests his own arrival. Q 6:85 enumerates them as a set, Q 19 runs incompatible origins, and the peace-formula is spoken over one at Q 19:15 and by the other about himself at Q 19:33. The joint cleanness is a structural requirement of the office, since an attestation is only as clean as both poles. The corpus's named instance of the same shape is Mūsā AS asking for Hārūn AS at Q 20:29-32: one *amr*, two persons, never collapsed. **And the earliest Gospel stages the merger and declines it**: three public identifications are reported at Mk 6:14-16 and Mk 8:28, John raised, Elijah returned, a prophet of old, and the narrator ratifies none, leaving a guilty king to supply the beheading himself.

**V · The distilled Christology, affirmed and addressed.** Affirmed, sixteen items: conception without a father, stated four times at Q 3:47, Q 19:20-21, Q 21:91, and Q 66:12; the title *al-Masīḥ* eleven times and never withdrawn, even inside the clauses condemning what was said about it; *kalima* at Q 3:39, Q 3:45, Q 4:171; *rūḥun minhu* at Q 4:171; the strengthening three times at Q 2:87, Q 2:253, Q 5:110; speech in cradle and maturity at Q 3:46; the Injīl with guidance and light at Q 5:46; the clay formed and breathed and the blind and leper healed and the dead raised, each with *bi-idhnī* attached at Q 3:49 and Q 5:110; purity declared before conception at Q 19:19; the taking and raising at Q 3:55 and Q 4:158; the threefold peace at Q 19:33; a place among the five of the solemn covenant at Q 33:7; compassion placed in his followers' hearts at Q 57:27; and knowledge of the Hour at Q 43:61.

**Addressed, and this edition restates the second column correctly.** The first edition wrote it as *denied* and counted removals. **The second column is not content and nothing in it is a subtraction.** It is three grammatical constructions, each addressed for a stated reason and each answered with a correct filling supplied elsewhere in the corpus and, in two of the three, in the adjacent clause. The convertible identity marked by the separative pronoun at Q 5:17 and Q 5:72, grounded on destructibility in the same verse and answered by the identical construction affirmed at Q 42:9 with the office correctly filled. The ordinal inside a count at Q 5:73 with the prohibition at Q 4:171, grounded on the corpus's only other ordinal-in-a-count at Q 53:20 and answered by threeness kept as structure in the very verse that bars the number. And offspring in the generative sense at Q 9:30, Q 19:35, Q 112:3, grounded on the absent consort at Q 6:101 and on *aṣ-Ṣamad* at Q 112:2 and answered by *kun fa-yakūn* in the next clause at Q 2:117 and Q 19:35, with the inference closed at Q 3:59 by the harder case of Adam AS. **The corpus does not remove a claim and leave a hole. It identifies a construction, states why it fails, and writes the correct form.** SC-063 carries this finding as its own coordinate and is not re-derived here. The named target at Q 5:116 is a triad of Allah ﷻ, ʿĪsā AS, and Maryam AS, which corresponds to no conciliar formula of any communion.

**VI · The Word applied, not re-derived.** SC-056 carries the doctrine and this coordinate uses it: the prior residence at Q 43:4, Q 13:39, Q 85:21-22, Q 56:77-78; the seven predicates, four of them divine names; the naming at Q 3:45 where the grammar breaks gender to follow the person; and the three registers with the count barred at Q 4:171. One clause is stated exactly here because the predicates leak without it: the casting, *alqāhā*, is an act and not a transfer of the predicated item, so what bears the seven is the Word in its residence and no vessel, Adamic parallels at Q 15:29 and Q 38:72 included. Q 3:59 is a likeness on the mode of origination alone and raises the anthropology to Word-and-Spirit rather than lowering ʿĪsā AS to dust; the differentia stands in the casting, direct and unmediated.

**VII · The Johannine consequence, and the Markan one that is stronger.** The Johannine clause is anarthrous and predicate-fronted, standing after *pros ton theon* which carries the article, and the Greek for a convertible equation, *ho theos ēn ho logos*, was available and was not written. On that verse the fence and the grammar fall in the same place. **And the earliest Gospel supplies a sharper instance the first edition did not carry.** At the baptism the voice quotes Psalm 2:7, *you are my son*, and the Psalm's next clause, *today I have begotten you*, is the clause Mark does not write. The Western text of Luke's parallel completes it, which shows both that the continuation was available and that a hand willing to write it existed. **What Q 19:35 fences six centuries later, the first evangelist had already declined to carry**, at the one moment in his book where heaven is speaking and could have said anything.

**VIII · The layer split and the crucifixion grammar.** Q 4:157 denies their accomplishment and their knowledge, with the ground stated in the verse, conjecture and not knowledge, and the subject *hum* throughout. The taking is assigned to the Source at Q 3:55 and Q 5:117, and ʿĪsā AS names his own death-day at Q 19:33. *Shubbiha* is from the root of *mutashābihāt* at Q 3:7: a resemblance-object presented to their sight, requiring no substitute body, only that their instrument reached the manifest layer while the event ran where their sight did not. The perishing layer and the remaining layer are assigned different words at Q 55:26-27 and Q 28:88, and the death-law's quantifier is bounded separately at Q 25:58 and Q 2:255. The surviving layer is universal and establishes no rank.

**IX · The corpus supplies what the earliest Gospel lacks.** Mark has no infancy narrative; Q 19:16-33 is a continuous account of the annunciation, the conception with the mother's protest preserved, the palm and the stream, the vow of silence, and the cradle speech. The corpus also carries the Injīl as given, the table at Q 5:112-115, the raising, and the sign of the Hour. And on one detail Mark and the corpus agree against the other three Gospels: **Mark alone says son of Mary with no father named at Mk 6:3**, and the corpus names him that way invariably, with the P45 variant noted rather than passed over.

**X · The bayān architecture, four verses.** The Word given at Q 55:2 before the receiver exists; the faculty of between-making given to *al-insān* as such at Q 55:4, the corpus keeping it lexically distinct from vocalisation at Q 27:16 and Q 41:21; the test issued at Q 4:82 in the register of internal coherence, with *yatadabbar* meaning to follow a thing to its terminus; and the completion deferred at Q 75:19, *thumma inna ʿalaynā bayānah*, so the test is real and its answer is not pre-supplied. **Deposit, instrument, challenge, deferral.** Beside it the register-indexed unity tokens: *wāḥid* at Q 37:4 spoken from inside the ranked line where counting is native, *aḥad* at Q 112:1 from the other side.

**XI · The transmission law that licenses the whole reading.** Fidelity is guaranteed in the corpus's severest language at Q 53:3-4, Q 10:15, and Q 69:44-46; comprehension is guaranteed nowhere and is instead the subject of commanded disclaimers at Q 6:50, Q 7:188, and Q 46:9 and a standing petition at Q 20:114, with the Khiḍr AS cycle at Q 18:65-82 the in-corpus precedent. **A verbatim standard preserves structure independently of any transmitter's or reader's grasp**, which is why recovered structure requires no claim that anyone unfolded it.

**XII · The reading itself, now continuous.** The first edition read eighteen sections. **The Master Edition reads the whole book**, Mk 1:1 through Mk 16:20, in ten movements, every passage quoted and read and tested with nothing skipped and nothing rearranged. Ninety-eight pericopes, none absent. The change is not one of scale only: a selective reading cannot exhibit an arrangement, and several of this coordinate's findings exist only because the connective passages were read. **The selection rule of the first edition is therefore retired rather than restated**, since nothing is selected, and with it the closure-rate caveat that the rule required.

**XIII · The seven threads, counted as they arrive, each a concordance or manuscript fact.** These are the Master Edition's own contribution and they are theorem-grade at the level of the count.

**The unnamed Agent.** Three formal reverent passives, *katharisthēti* at Mk 1:41, *apekatestathē* at Mk 3:5, *hētoimastai* at Mk 10:40, with the convention running continuously beneath: *aphientai*, *aparthē*, *dedotai*, *metemorphōthē*, *ou dothēsetai*, *anakekylistai*, *ēgerthē*, and *anelēmphthē* in the appended ending. **Every one is a place the corpus writes the Name in the active.** Mark leaves the blank; the corpus fills it.

**Strength, five occurrences of one root, descending.** *Ischyroteros* announced at Mk 1:7, the strong man bound at Mk 3:27, none with strength to subdue at Mk 5:4, the disciples *ouk ischysan* at Mk 9:18, and Peter *ouk ischysas* one hour at Mk 14:37. **Strength is never held in this book; it is granted**, which is the corpus's law at Q 40:78 and why the mockers at Mk 15:31 state the doctrine when they say he cannot save himself.

**The spitting, five, crossing at the midpoint.** Mk 7:33 and Mk 8:23 as the medium of healing, Mk 10:34 predicted, Mk 14:65 and Mk 15:19 enacted as humiliation.

**Four Aramaic retentions, all moments of address.** *Talitha koum*, *ephphatha*, *Abba*, *Elōi Elōi lema sabachthani*, each glossed for a Greek reader, two spoken to the afflicted and two upward. **The fourth is preserved so faithfully that the mishearing is preserved with it**, a mistake possible only in the Aramaic actually used. A text recast in Greek categories does not keep the source language precisely at the points of address.

**Six intercalations**, at Mk 3:20-35, Mk 5:21-43, Mk 6:7-30, Mk 11:12-21, Mk 14:1-11, and Mk 14:53-72, with a seventh instance of the same instinct running across pericope boundaries at Mk 12:38 through Mk 13:2: widows devoured, a widow giving all, the building thrown down, with no connecting word.

**The transit ledger, three grades.** Crossed whole: the sandal-thong at Mk 1:7, and the camel through the needle's eye at Mk 10:25 against Q 7:40, same two objects and same argumentative position. Crossed in part: the shepherd, Esther's half-kingdom inverted at Mk 6:23, the cloaks of 2 Kings 9:13 without the enthronement, the silent servant, and the wrath-cup, which the corpus does not take up since every *kaʾs* in it is the garden's drink. Declined by Mark: the scroll rolled at Isaiah 34:4 which the corpus takes up at Q 21:104, Zechariah's oracle staged and unquoted, and the begetting clause of Psalm 2:7.

**Eleven roughnesses, every one running rough toward smooth in the derived texts.** The composite citation filed under Isaiah, the wrong high priest, *ouk edynato* at Nazareth which Matthew converts to *did not*, the permitted staff and sandals, *prayer* alone at Mk 9:29 to which later hands add fasting, the crowd blessing the kingdom which Matthew moves to the son and Luke to the king, Zechariah unquoted, **no command to repeat the supper**, the severed ear left unhealed, the Aramaic cry left harder with its mishearing, Pilate's surprise at the speed of the death, and the ending on a conjunction. **The direction of change is the argument**, and it is the same property the corpus exhibits when a messenger's file contains *ʿabasa wa-tawallā* addressed to himself in the third person.

**XIV · The *ʿinda* chain, and the Greek link.** What is with Him: the Mother of the Book at Q 13:39, what remains at Q 16:96, the provision found with Maryam AS at Q 3:37, the signs at Q 6:109 which is the answer to the sign-demand Mark refuses in the passive at Mk 8:12, the appointed term at Q 6:2, the martyrs *ʿinda rabbihim* at Q 3:169 which is the corpus's form of the burning-bush argument at Mk 12:26-27, and the Hour four times at Q 7:187, Q 31:34, Q 41:47, Q 43:85. **Mark reaches for the preposition once, in Greek, at the exact point where human capacity has been shown to end**: *para tō theō panta dynata* at Mk 10:27, spoken when the disciples ask who then can be saved.

**XV · What this edition revises in the first, named rather than repaired quietly.** Three items, per the Genealogy Law.

**One, the corrections move from seven to eight**, and the eighth is Mk 14:24, the blood of the covenant. Sinai throws the blood *upon* the people at Exodus 24:8 and Leviticus 17:11 bars its ingestion with the reason stated; the corpus bars it three times and then states the principle at Q 22:37, *neither their flesh nor their blood reaches Allah ﷻ, but the reverence from you reaches Him*. **Five of the eight are now the same operation the corpus performs on itself**, and Q 22:37 is addressed to the corpus's own pilgrims inside the passage that commands the rite. SC-062 carries the arc as its own coordinate.

**Two, the non-closure count moves from two to three**, and one of the original two is resolved. Mk 12:25, the marriage-clause, is no longer exhibited as a divergence: **Mark's *gamousin* and *gamizontai* deny the human transaction and the corpus's *zawwajnāhum* at Q 44:54 and Q 52:20 assigns the pairing to the Lord with He as the only subject that verb ever takes there.** The contracting ends and the gift remains; the two are halves of one sentence. What replaces it in the open column is the interior of the raised state, which Q 32:17 states is withheld, and a statement that content is withheld is not a description of it. The three now open are the interior of the cry at Mk 15:34, the interior of the raised state at Mk 12:25, and the referent of *these things* at Mk 13:30. Mk 9:1 is folded into the third.

**Three, the second column of the Christology is retyped**, from *denied* and counted as removals to *addressed* and answered with fillings, per movement V. The old phrasing invited the reading that something was taken and nothing put back, which the corpus's own adjacent clauses refute.

**XVI · The counterfeit law, with its rival stated.** A figure with no fault-file is maximally claimable because nothing in it must be reproduced; a figure with one is not, because no impostor writes his own rebukes. The corpus adds two locks for its own bearer, the sealed office at Q 33:40 and the anti-cult clause at Q 3:144, and either would explain the absence of claimants on its own, so for that case the fault-file is a consistency observation and not a demonstrated mechanism. **Where the law does independent work is on texts**: the longer ending after Mk 16:8 is the first documented instance, a raw deposit ending on a conjunction, then completed versions circulating as correct, **and completed in more than one way**, since the manuscripts carry a shorter ending as well and some carry both. The multiplicity is the evidence. **And the dilution is visible in the syntax and not only in the apparatus**: the appended sign-list at Mk 16:17-18 promises capacities to believers with no permission-clause attached, against Q 3:49 which attaches *bi-idhni Allāh* four times in one verse. **The interpolating hand still wrote *anelēmphthē*, passive**, which shows how deep the convention ran even in an addition.

**XVII · Fences, [X] on the named inflations, beside the faces and terminal for none.** The convertible identity in either direction, whether asserted or inferred from the divine-kind predicates. The count. Reading the fault-silence as ontological exemption rather than as the voluntariness guard. Merging the two-place office into one figure, which deletes the relation Q 3:39 requires. Treating the surviving layer at Q 4:157 as a unique exemption, when *min rūḥī* is universal. Reading the historical lineage as descent rather than as convergence of independent strata. Claiming any of it settles the identity of ʿĪsā AS, which the apophatic register cannot carry and the orientation-blind lock cannot rank. The *periklytos* emendation, which no Greek witness supports and which Q 61:6 does not need. **And, added in this edition, reading the seven threads as evidence of dependence in either direction**: the counts establish properties of one text and correspondences with another, and a correspondence is not a channel.

**XVIII · Perimeter and provenance.** The coordinate adjudicates no identity: that question is beyond the instrument, and an instrument returning a verdict there would report its operator. Every post-canonical corpus of every tradition enters as witness only and carries no conclusion, the classical tafsir corpus included. The historical lineage stands around the argument and never inside it. **No new philology, no new mathematics, no new textual discovery: the contribution is the arrangement**, every ingredient available to any reader with a concordance, a lexicon, and a standard grammar. Six audit rounds are recorded in the first published edition and the four durable repairs are carried forward; the Master Edition's own repairs are the three named at movement XV. **Four coordinates were harvested out of this reading and seated on their own attestations**, SC-061 through SC-064, and this coordinate does not re-derive them. ΔM equal to zero.

### SC-061 · CN-PSP-QUL-DELETION-01 · The Deleted Imperative · The Distributional Proof of the Unbrokered Channel at Q 2:186

Status: SEALED-RESIDENT · Book IV, the Word and its Registers · [⟀ T] on the *yasʾalūnaka* distribution and the deletion, a concordance fact reproducible by any reader · [⟀ T] on the first-person answer and the absent imperative in the received text · [⟀ S] on the reading of the deletion as structural rather than stylistic · [⟀ S] on the structural reading, the binding-term removal · [⟀ CO] on the cross-corpus mediation census, corroboration-grade and load-bearing on nothing · [X] on the three named inflations · ΔM = 0
Source: the architect's observation in session, that prayer in the corpus runs on a single voice, tested against the surah's own distribution and found to be carried by an absence
Depends: CN-PSP-KALIMA-MASTER-01, whose Word-doctrine seats the register in which speech is routed · CN-PSP-GHAYB-MASTER-01, under which the interior of the address is not adjudicated here · APEX-PSP-GENEALOGY, whose Face-L screens govern the term-ledger below
Governs: the grammatical ground of the unbrokered channel, and the exact scope of what that ground licenses

**I · The pattern, stated before the exception.** Sūrat al-Baqarah carries a dense run of question-verses built on one formula: *yasʾalūnaka ʿan*, they ask you about, followed by a topic, followed by an imperative addressed to the Prophet ﷺ that routes the answer through him. The new moons at Q 2:189, *yasʾalūnaka ʿani al-ahillah, qul hiya mawāqītu li-l-nās*. What to spend at Q 2:215, *yasʾalūnaka mādhā yunfiqūn, qul mā anfaqtum min khayr*. Fighting in the sacred month at Q 2:217, *yasʾalūnaka ʿani al-shahri al-ḥarāmi qitālin fīh, qul qitālun fīhi kabīr*. Wine and gambling at Q 2:219, *yasʾalūnaka ʿani al-khamri wa-l-maysir, qul fīhimā ithmun kabīr*, and in the same verse a second question with *wa-yasʾalūnaka mādhā yunfiqūn, quli al-ʿafw*. Orphans at Q 2:220, *wa-yasʾalūnaka ʿani al-yatāmā, qul iṣlāḥun lahum khayr*. Menstruation at Q 2:222, *wa-yasʾalūnaka ʿani al-maḥīḍ, qul huwa adhā*. Seven questions, seven imperatives, one rule with no exception in the run.

**II · The exception, and it is the only one.** Q 2:186 sits inside the same surah and inside the same formula-family, and it does not carry the imperative. *Wa-idhā saʾalaka ʿibādī ʿannī fa-innī qarīb, ujību daʿwata al-dāʿi idhā daʿān.* And when My servants ask you about Me, then I am near; I answer the call of the caller when he calls Me. **There is no *qul*.** The verse does not instruct the messenger to say anything. It answers over him, in the first person, and every pronoun in the answer belongs to the One asked about: *innī*, *ujību*, *daʿān*.

**III · Four differences, and they are cumulative.** First, the imperative is absent where the run supplies it seven times. Second, the verb shifts from the plural *yasʾalūnaka* to the singular-collective *saʾalaka*, and the askers are named *ʿibādī*, My servants, a possessive that puts them on the answerer's side of the relation before the answer begins. Third, the answer is in the first person throughout, where the routed answers are in the second and third. Fourth, the topic is not a ruling but a Person, *ʿannī*, about Me, and this is the only question in the run whose object is the One being asked about rather than a matter He legislates. **The deletion tracks the topic exactly.**

**IV · What the deletion is not.** It is not a scribal variant; the received text is uniform here and the *qirāʾāt* record no variation on the presence of the imperative at this verse. It is not a stylistic economy, because the surrounding verses are longer and carry the imperative anyway. And it is not an isolated flourish, because the corpus repeats the structure it enacts. *Wa-naḥnu aqrabu ilayhi min ḥabli al-warīd*, and We are nearer to him than his jugular vein (Q 50:16). *Udʿūnī astajib lakum*, call upon Me and I will answer you (Q 40:60), with no intermediary named on either side of the transaction. *Wa-idhā saʾalaka* deletes the messenger from the syntax; Q 50:16 removes distance from the image; Q 40:60 removes any third party from the contract. **Three registers, one removal.**

**V · The term-ledger, run at Seal L on original definitions.** *Qul* is an imperative of *qāla*, and in the corpus's messenger-verses it marks delegated transmission: the messenger is told what to say and says it. *Qarīb* is proximity, from √q-r-b, and its use here is not spatial but relational, the same root that gives *muqarrabūn*, those brought near, at Q 3:45. *Daʿwah* and *dāʿī* and *daʿān* are three forms of √d-ʿ-w in one clause, which is the corpus's ordinary way of sealing a subject by saturation. **No term in the verse requires an intermediary and one term that would supply one has been removed.**

**VI · What this ties, and one cross-corpus note carried at its grade.** The finding is complete inside the corpus and needs nothing external. Recorded beside it, at comparative grade and load-bearing on nothing, is that the earliest Gospel independently keeps the same channel open in narrative rather than in syntax: its prophet withdraws alone before dawn to petition (Mk 1:35), prays alone on a mountain (Mk 6:46), tells men who failed that the missing element was prayer (Mk 9:29), and dies with an address on his lips (Mk 15:34). And when a scribe answers well he is told *ou makran ei*, you are not far (Mk 12:34), which is the Greek of the adjective this verse uses of the One asked about. **The observation is comparative and it seals nothing.** SC-058 holds the Mark reading and this coordinate does not re-derive it.

**VI-a · The cross-corpus census of the same channel, each tradition read from inside itself.** The finding is complete within the corpus and is fortified, not grounded, by what follows.

**Hebrew Bible.** The mediated channel is legislated and its limit is legislated with it. Israel at the mountain asks for a mediator in so many words, *dabber attah ʿimmanu we-nishmaʿah, we-al yedabber ʿimmanu Elohim pen namut*, you speak with us and we will hear, but let not God speak with us lest we die (Exodus 20:19), and the request is granted for the law. **And the same corpus keeps a channel the mediator does not stand in.** Hannah prays at Shiloh with her lips moving and no sound, and the priest standing beside her misreads her and is corrected (1 Samuel 1:13-17): the officiant is present, unhearing, and not in the circuit. Solomon's dedication prayer routes the whole nation's petition toward the house and past every officer in it, *and hear Thou in heaven Thy dwelling place, and when Thou hearest, forgive* (1 Kings 8:30). And the promise of the new covenant abolishes the teaching-mediation by name: *and they shall teach no more every man his neighbour... for they shall all know Me, from the least of them unto the greatest* (Jeremiah 31:34).

**Aramaic and Syriac witness.** The Targumim, which insert *memra* as a buffer at hundreds of anthropomorphic points, are the clearest measure of how strong the mediating instinct was in that milieu, and they are the reason the unmediated verses stand out where they survive.

**New Testament.** The same double structure. A mediator is named at 1 Timothy 2:5 and the temple veil is torn from the top in the earliest Gospel at Mk 15:38, and between those two the Synoptic prayer is taught with no officiant in it, addressed directly, in the second person plural, *Our Father*. And in the earliest Gospel the practice is narrated four times before it is ever explained: alone before dawn (Mk 1:35), alone on the mountain (Mk 6:46), *Abba* in the garden (Mk 14:36), and the cry from the cross (Mk 15:34). **All four are addresses with nobody in the room.**

**Sanskrit and Pali.** The Vedic sacrifice is priest-mediated by construction, and the Upaniṣadic turn is precisely the interiorisation of it, *tat tvam asi* at Chāndogya 6.8.7 relocating the whole transaction inward. The Pali *Kālāma Sutta* removes teacher-authority as a warrant, *mā anussavena*, not by report, not by lineage, not by the authority of a teacher. Comparative, and neither is a theistic address-verse, so both are recorded as parallel motion and not as parallel doctrine.

**What the census shows.** Every tradition that legislates a mediator also preserves an unmediated address, and no tradition legislates the mediator into the address itself. **The corpus's contribution is that it states the exception in syntax rather than in narrative or in promise**, by deleting the one word that would have routed it.

**VI-b · The structural reading, and it is the coordinate's geometric spine.** A complete claim carries three registers: what stands, what moves, and how the two bind.

**What stands.** Two parties and, in the seven routed verses, a third: the questioner, the messenger, the One asked. At Q 2:186 the third slot is occupied by the topic itself, since the question is *ʿannī*, about Me.

**What moves.** The question travels and the answer travels back. In the routed verses the motion is two-legged, up through the messenger and down through him again, and the imperative *qul* is the hinge that makes the second leg happen.

**How they bind.** The imperative. Delete it and the two-leg path has no hinge, and the answer arrives on one leg. **That is what the verse does, and the deletion is therefore not a stylistic economy but the removal of the binding term itself.**

The reading closes because the three registers stand apart and compose: the parties are distinct, the motion is real, and the binding term is present in seven verses and absent in one, measurably. **And the geometry names why a mediator cannot be inserted here even in principle**: an intermediary is a term that stands between two others, and a question whose object is one of the two others has no between left to occupy. The corpus does not argue this. It writes a sentence in which the position does not exist.

**VII · The verdict.** The corpus routes every ruling through its messenger and deletes him from the one answer whose subject is Himself. The deletion is a fact of distribution, checkable in an afternoon with a concordance, and it is theorem-grade at that level. The reading of the deletion as the syntactic form of an unbrokered channel is structural, and it is stated at that grade and not above it.

**VIII · Fences, [X] on the named inflations.** One, **the removal is scoped to the answer about Him and licenses nothing else.** The corpus routes law, ritual, obedience, arbitration, and the *ṣalāh*'s own forms through the messenger without exception, *man yuṭiʿi al-rasūla fa-qad aṭāʿa Allāh* at Q 4:80, and any reading that generalises the deletion into a doctrine of intermediary-free religion is barred. Two, **the finding is not a claim about the interior of prayer**, whose content is routed out of band under the apophatic constitution and adjudicated nowhere here. Three, **the count is the fact and the inference is not the fact**, and the two are typed separately on the page so no reader mistakes the second for the first.

### SC-064 · CN-PSP-BAR-ENASHA-01 · The Human One · The Recipient-Title Across Six Corpora, the Candidates Who Filled It, and the Office That Is Not a Nature

Status: SEALED-RESIDENT · Book IV, the Word and its Registers · [⟀ T] on the grammar of conferral at Daniel 7:14, on the Ezekiel vocative census, and on the Q 18:110 and Q 43:59 reductions, all concordance facts · [⟀ S] on the recipient-invariant across the six corpora and on the office-not-nature reading · [⟀ S] on the candidate-instability datum · [⟀ T] on the conferral-verb census across Hebrew, Aramaic, Greek, and Arabic · [⟀ S] on the grant-structure reading that identifies the position as an office · [⟀ T] on the *malik* distribution and the never-conferred construct *malik al-nās* · [⟀ S] on the both-ends reading, the receiver titled in the earlier corpora and the Giver titled at Q 114 · [⟀ T] on the √h-d-y split, the active participle a divine name and the passive the creature's · [⟀ S] on the forward run, which types the position and populates it not at all · [⟀ CO] on the witness material, corroboration by multiplicity of roads and load-bearing on nothing · the occupancy and the timing held open, the aperture located and uncrossed · [X] on the seven named inflations · every identification of an office-holder routed out of band, no token · the future complex neither forecast nor adjudicated · post-canonical material witness-only throughout · ΔM = 0
Source: the architect's instruction to unbind the coordinate from the Mark-Quran pair and run it across the Hebrew Bible, the Dead Sea material, the apocrypha and pseudepigrapha, the New Testament, the Gnostic corpus, and the Quran, with the named candidates Enoch, Melchizedek, David, Solomon, Elijah, ʿĪsā AS, Muhammad ﷺ, and the later Mahdī-ʿĪsā complex
Depends: CN-PSP-GHAYB-MASTER-01 and CN-PSP-NETI-NETI-01, whose fences bar every identification this coordinate declines to make · CN-PSP-MAHDI-PARADOX (SC-054), which already holds the future complex and is not re-derived here · SC-058, the Clean Gospel of Mark Master, whose Markan material this coordinate draws on and does not repeat · APEX-PSP-GENEALOGY, whose bedrock ordering governs the six-corpus survey · SC-076, which seats the second warrant, the earth-domain, the service inversion, and the root bridge beside this coordinate and re-derives none of it
Governs: the reading of a title whose exaltation moves and whose grammar does not

**I · The thesis, stated first so the survey can be checked against it.** Across six corpora separated by a millennium and more, in four languages, in communities that anathematised one another, **the title *son of man* keeps one grammatical property: it names a recipient.** The exaltation attached to the figure varies enormously, from a prophet addressed as mortal, to a heavenly figure enthroned, to an emanated cosmic Anthropos. **The receiving does not vary.** At maximum exaltation the corpora still write the figure as one to whom something is given, and the Quran's terminal statement of the same figure-class, *in huwa illā ʿabdun anʿamnā ʿalayh*, is the endpoint of a line that runs the whole length of the material.

**II · Corpus one, the Hebrew Bible, and it establishes the baseline.** *Ben adam*, son of man, is the ordinary Hebrew for a human being, and Ezekiel carries it as a vocative more than ninety times, always as address from the Lord to the prophet, always at the moment of commission: *ben adam, ʿamod ʿal ragleykhā*, son of man, stand on your feet (Ezekiel 2:1). **The vocative marks the addressee's mortality against the One addressing him**, and it is the term's baseline sense in the whole tradition. The Psalter uses it the same way, *mah enosh ki tizkerennu u-ven adam ki tifqedennu*, what is man that You are mindful of him, and the son of man that You visit him (Psalm 8:4), where the phrase is explicitly the small term in a comparison with the Maker.

**III · Corpus one, second layer, Daniel 7, where the exaltation begins and the grammar does not change.** *Ḥāzeh hăwēt... wa-ʾărū ʿim ʿănānē shəmayyā kəvar ʾenāsh ʾātēh hăwāh*, one like a son of man coming with the clouds of heaven, and he came to the Ancient of Days (Daniel 7:13). The Aramaic *kəvar ʾenāsh* is the Aramaic of the Hebrew phrase, and the figure is exalted as far as that literature exalts anything. **And the next verse is the whole point.** *Wə-lēh yəhīv shŏlṭān wīqār u-malkhū*, and to him **was given** dominion and glory and a kingdom (Daniel 7:14). *Yəhīv* is a passive of *yəhav*, to give. **The most exalted picture the Hebrew scriptures offer of this figure is a picture of conferral**, with the Giver in the room and named two verses earlier. Theorem-grade as a reading of the verse's own morphology.

**IV · Corpus two, the pseudepigrapha, and the first named candidate.** The Parables of Enoch, 1 Enoch 37 through 71, carry a Son of Man who is chosen before creation, hidden, seated on a throne of glory, and revealed to judge, and the same section closes by identifying that figure with **Enoch himself**, the man who *walked with God, and was not, for God took him* (Genesis 5:24). Two things are load-bearing and both are typed. **The dating is contested**, with the Parables absent from the Qumran Enoch manuscripts and generally placed around the turn of the era, so nothing is built here on their priority to any other corpus. **And the identification is a filling**: a human being is placed into the office, which is precisely the datum this coordinate is gathering. Conditional grade on the dating, structural on the reading.

**V · Corpus three, the Dead Sea material, and the second and third candidates.** The community's expectation is stated in its own hand and it is plural: *ʿad boʾ nāvīʾ u-meshīḥē ʾAharon we-Yisraʾel*, until the coming of a prophet and the anointed ones of Aaron and Israel (1QS 9:11), with the Damascus Document and 1QSa carrying the same two-office structure. **A priestly anointed and a royal anointed, side by side, and the office is therefore already known to be fillable by more than one person at once.** 11Q13 seats **Melchizedek** as a heavenly deliverer executing judgment, drawing Psalm 82 and Isaiah 61 onto a figure whose scriptural warrant is three verses in Genesis and one in a Psalm. 4Q521 lists the works that accompany the anointed one's coming, healing the wounded and raising the dead and preaching to the poor, without naming him. **And what is not there should be said plainly**: no securely readable text of that library presents a *suffering* messiah, the single candidate being a damaged fragment, 4Q285, whose broken line reads either way. Theorem-grade on what the manuscripts contain, and the absence is recorded as an absence and not as a proof.

**VI · The other candidates, and the pattern they make.** **David** is the office's royal anchor and the source of the lineage-claim, and Psalm 110's *neʾum YHWH la-adoni* is the text on which the later argument turned, with the two Hebrew words flattened into one Greek *kyrios* and the riddle born in translation. **Solomon** carries the wisdom-and-dominion face, and the corpus's own account of him is a study in conferral: *rabbi ghfir lī wa-hab lī mulkan lā yanbaghī li-aḥadin min baʿdī*, my Lord, forgive me and grant me a dominion (Q 38:35), a kingdom asked for and given, and the wind and the jinn subjected by grant. **Elijah** carries the returning-forerunner face, from Malachi 4:5, and the corpus's Ilyās AS stands in the prophetic roll at Q 37:123-132 with the same *wa-taraknā ʿalayhi fī al-ākhirīn* the surah gives four others. **ʿĪsā AS** and **Muhammad ﷺ** carry the two files this codex already holds. **And the pattern across all seven is the datum**: the office is filled and refilled, the identifications shift by community and by century, and no two of these communities agree on who holds it. **An office that is filled and refilled is an office, not a nature.** Structural, and it is this coordinate's second central finding.

**VII · Corpus four, the New Testament, and the self-designation.** *Ho huios tou anthrōpou* is the dominant self-designation in the Synoptic material, and in the earliest Gospel it is introduced twice in disputes about **warrant**, at Mk 2:10 on authority to forgive on earth and at Mk 2:28 on lordship over an ordinance. Its exalted uses quote Daniel and Psalm 110 together, at Mk 14:62, *seated at the right hand of Power and coming with the clouds*, which is a seat conferred beside another and a dominion the source-verse says was given. **And the same Gospel places the figure below a line drawn by knowledge**, at Mk 13:32, *not even the Son, but only the Father*. The Aramaic under the Greek is *bar enasha*, the human one, which is the phrase this coordinate is named for. SC-058 holds the reading; the grammar is recorded here.

**VIII · Corpus five, the Gnostic material, read as reception and never as witness.** In the Nag Hammadi corpus and the systems the heresiologists describe, the Anthropos becomes a cosmic principle and the Son of Man an emanation of it, and in several systems the human Jesus becomes the vehicle of a higher Christ who departs before the passion. **Two observations and both are typed.** First, the trajectory is the reverse of the Semitic baseline: a term whose original force was *mortal, addressed, commissioned* has become a term for a pre-cosmic emanation. Second, **that reversal is exactly what the Quranic fence names**, since the corpus's *basharun mithlukum* insists on the term's baseline sense against every inflation of it. Post-canonical and sectarian, admitted witness-only under the Provenance Firewall, and load-bearing on nothing.

**IX · Corpus six, the Quran, where the line terminates.** The corpus carries no *ibn al-insān* as a title of the human figure, and the reason is not that the office is absent. **The office is titled on the other side**, which movement IX-a sets out. On the human side the corpus makes the baseline sense a commanded declaration: *qul innamā anā basharun mithlukum yūḥā ilayya*, say: I am only a human like you, to whom it is revealed (Q 18:110, and Q 41:6). **The term of address becomes the term of self-description, in the imperative.** And of the figure the other corpora most exalted, the corpus's terminal statement is *in huwa illā ʿabdun anʿamnā ʿalayhi wa-jaʿalnāhu mathalan li-banī Isrāʾīl*, he was only a servant upon whom We placed favour and whom We made an example for the Children of Israel (Q 43:59), with the placing and the making both Another's verbs. **The corpus is not lowering the figure. It is stating the grammar the whole line was already written in**, from Ezekiel's vocative through Daniel's *yehiv* to the seat at the right hand that is by definition not the throne.

**IX-a · The office has a title, and Sūrat al-Nās carries it.** The absence of a human-side title is not an absence of office-language. **The corpus titles the relation, and it seats the titles on the Giver.** The closing surah names three of them in three consecutive verses, each in construct with the same governed noun:

*Qul aʿūdhu bi-rabbi al-nās · maliki al-nās · ilāhi al-nās* (Q 114:1-3). **Rabb al-Nās**, the Lord of mankind, the sustaining and nurturing relation, √r-b-b. **Malik al-Nās**, the Sovereign of mankind, the relation of dominion and rule, √m-l-k. **Ilāh al-Nās**, the God of mankind, the relation of worship, √ʾ-l-h. Three titles, one governed term, ascending from provision through rule to worship, and every one of them is a title *of relation to the human race* rather than a description of essence.

**And the distribution of *malik* is the finding.** The corpus confers kingship on creatures freely and by name. Ṭālūt is made king by explicit divine appointment, *inna Allāha qad baʿatha lakum Ṭālūta malikā* (Q 2:247). Egypt has its *malik* throughout the Yūsuf AS narrative (Q 12:43, 12:50, 12:54). A king seizing ships stands behind the Khiḍr episode (Q 18:79). Sulaymān AS asks for and receives *mulkan lā yanbaghī li-aḥadin min baʿdī* (Q 38:35). And the Children of Israel are told *wa-jaʿalakum mulūkan*, He made you kings (Q 5:20). **Kingship over a domain is granted, repeatedly, to named human beings.**

**And the three are not uniformly reserved. They form a graded ladder, and the grading is the finding.** Each title names the absolute holder of a relation, and the question the corpus answers differently for each is whether that relation admits a created, delegated shadow.

***Ilāh al-Nās* admits none.** Every appearance of a creature in that position in the corpus is a condemnation: *a-raʾayta man ittakhadha ilāhahu hawāh*, have you seen the one who takes his desire as his god (Q 25:43, and Q 45:23), and *lā tajʿal maʿa Allāhi ilāhan ākhar* (Q 17:22). **The worship-relation has no shadow and no vicegerent, and this is the one seat in the ladder that is closed.**

***Rabb al-Nās* has a named and licensed shadow in the corpus's own commanded prayer.** *Wa-qul rabbi irḥamhumā kamā rabbayānī ṣaghīrā*, and say: my Lord, have mercy on them as they **reared** me when I was small (Q 17:24). *Rabbayānī* is √r-b-b, the root of the title, used of two human beings, inside a prayer the corpus instructs the believer to make. **The rearing-function has a created form and the corpus names it in the same breath as the divine one**, with the preposition *kamā*, as, marking the likeness and the distance at once. And *rabb* in the human sense of master runs through the Yūsuf AS narrative as reported speech (Q 12:42, Q 12:50).

***Malik al-Nās* has a shadow, and it is conferred more often and more explicitly than any other office in the corpus.** *Innī jāʿilun fī al-arḍi khalīfah*, I am placing on the earth a successor (Q 2:30), said before any prophet is sent. *Yā Dāwūdu innā jaʿalnāka khalīfatan fī al-arḍi fa-ḥkum bayna al-nāsi bi-l-ḥaqq*, O Dāwūd, We have made you a successor on the earth, so judge **among mankind** with truth (Q 38:26), and the very next clause is the warning that travels with the grant, *wa-lā tattabiʿ al-hawā fa-yuḍillaka ʿan sabīli Allāh*. **That verse confers rule over *al-nās* by name, on a named man, in the corpus's own words.** With Ṭālūt raised *malikan* (Q 2:247), a people made *mulūkan* (Q 5:20), *ulū al-amri minkum* owed obedience with a precedence-clause attached (Q 4:59), and *aʾimmatan yahdūna bi-amrinā* (Q 32:24).

**So the correction to the first reading of this movement is exact and it is owed.** What is reserved is not the office. **What is reserved is the ownership**, and the corpus separates the two words: *quli Allāhumma Mālika al-mulki tuʾtī al-mulka man tashāʾu wa-tanziʿu al-mulka mimman tashāʾ*, say: O Allah ﷻ, Owner of dominion, You give dominion to whom You will and You withdraw dominion from whom You will (Q 3:26). **The Owner of dominion is the one title in the field that is not itself a grant**, and every other kingship in the corpus is an object of the two verbs in that verse, given and withdrawn. *Mālik*, owner, is the active participle of possession; *malik*, king, is the one who rules. **The corpus withholds the owning and distributes the ruling**, and the two verbs of Q 3:26, *tuʾtī* and *tanziʿu*, are the proof that the ruling is in circulation: a thing that is given and withdrawn is a thing that is held by others.

**And therefore the office is open, not closed.** As long as there are *nās* there is the ordering of *nās*, and the corpus installs the office before it installs anything else: the *khalīfa* announcement at Q 2:30 stands before the first prophet, before the first book, before the first law. **Prophethood is an office and messengership is an office, and this one is older than both**, because a household needs ordering before a community needs a scripture. The first holder in the human register is the one the corpus names with the title's own root: the parent who *reared*. **There is no conflict in this and the corpus states why there is none.** A holder who holds by grant is not a rival to the Giver; he is the grant, in operation. That is the whole content of *khalīfa*, and it is why Q 38:26 can confer rule over mankind in one clause and warn against desire in the next without contradiction.

**So the corpus completes the invariant from the other end, and the ladder is what makes it precise.** Where a relation admits a shadow, the shadow is conferred on recipients and carries a permission-clause; where it admits none, the seat is closed and every occupant is condemned. **One of the three is closed and two are open**, and the survey's whole cast of candidates stands in the open ones.

**The sharper statement of the coordinate's thesis.** The earlier corpora title the receiver: *ben adam*, *bar enash*, *mashiaḥ*, *ho huios tou anthrōpou*, every one of them a name for the one acted upon. **The Quran titles the Giver in the same relation**: Rabb, Malik, Ilāh, each in construct with *al-nās*, and leaves the human side at *basharun mithlukum* and *ʿabd*. Both ends of the grant are named across the survey, and no corpus in it names the same end twice. **The office is real, the office is titled, and the title sits where the giving sits.**

One further note, recorded at its grade. The relation-titles of Q 114 stand in a surah about *seeking refuge*, which is the posture of a receiver, and the threat named in it is the whisperer *who whispers in the breasts of mankind*. **The three titles are invoked by a creature in the act of asking**, so even the naming of the Giver is placed inside the receiving. Structural.

**X · The invariant, gathered.** Six corpora, four languages, roughly a thousand years, and communities in mutual anathema. Ezekiel: addressed as mortal. Daniel: *yehiv*, given. Enoch: chosen, hidden, revealed, and the choosing is Another's. Qumran: anointed, which is a passive of a rite performed on a person. The Gospels: authority *given*, glory that is *his Father's*, seats *prepared* by another, knowledge withheld. Gnostic: the one system that breaks the pattern, and it breaks it by leaving the Semitic term behind. Quran: *ʿabd* upon whom favour was placed, with the office titled on the Giver's side as Rabb and Malik and Ilāh of mankind. **The exaltation moves the whole distance from a prophet on his feet to a figure on the clouds, and the receiving never moves at all.** And where the receiving is not titled, the giving is: **the survey names both ends of one grant and never the same end twice.**

**X-a · The conferral-verbs, gathered as a census, because the invariant is carried by verbs and not by titles.** This is the coordinate's hardest evidence and it is checkable in every language.

**Hebrew and Aramaic.** *Yəhīv*, was given, Daniel 7:14, passive of *yəhav*, with the dominion and the glory and the kingdom all its objects. *Mashaḥ*, anointed, always with a human or divine anointer as subject and the anointed as object, of Saul at 1 Samuel 10:1, of David at 1 Samuel 16:13, of Cyrus at Isaiah 45:1. *Laqaḥ*, took, of Enoch at Genesis 5:24, *ki laqaḥ oto Elohim*. *Natan*, gave, of Solomon's wisdom at 1 Kings 3:12, *hinneh natatti lekha lev ḥakham*.

**Greek.** *Edothē*, it was given, the aorist passive that runs through the apocalyptic material. *Hētoimastai*, it has been prepared, of the seats at Mk 10:40. *Paredothē*, was handed over. And the glory at Mk 8:38 is *tou patros autou*, a genitive of possession that assigns it elsewhere.

**Arabic.** *Ātaynā*, We gave, of the Book and of wisdom, at Q 19:12 of Yaḥyā AS and Q 19:30 of ʿĪsā AS. *Ayyadnā*, We strengthened, three times of the same figure. *Anʿamnā ʿalayh*, We placed favour upon him, at Q 43:59. *Jaʿalnā*, We made, of his being an example. *Wahabnā*, We granted. **And the permission-clause itself, *bi-idhnī*, four times inside one verse at Q 3:49**, which is the invariant compressed into a single ayah.

**The census result.** In every corpus the exalting term is a passive or a transitive with the Giver as subject. **There is no corpus in the survey in which the figure's dominion is predicated of him with an intransitive verb of possession.** Theorem-grade as a distributional claim across the material surveyed, and the one construction that would break it, an active *he took* or a stative *he had*, does not occur.

**X-b · The structural reading, and it is what makes the invariant a finding rather than a list.** Three registers.

**What stands.** A giver, a receiver, and a thing given. Three terms, and every corpus in the survey supplies all three.

**What moves.** The conferral. And in every corpus the direction is single: downward from the giver to the receiver, never upward and never lateral.

**How they bind.** The passive voice, or the transitive with the giver as subject. **That is the binding term, and it is the same term in four languages.**

**The reading closes, and the closing is what identifies an office.** Three distinct terms with a directional binding is the structure of a grant. Collapse the giver into the receiver and the sentence stops parsing, because a thing cannot be given to the one who gives it; that is not a theological error but a compositional one, the same failure the frame-check identifies at SC-063. **So the title names a position in a grant-structure, and a position in a grant-structure is exactly what an office is.**

**And the instability of the fillings is the confirming datum.** Enoch, Melchizedek, David, Solomon, Elijah, and the two prophetic files have all been placed in this position by one community or another, and no two of those communities agree. **A position that different communities fill differently is a position and not a person**, and a nature is not the kind of thing that can be filled by candidates. The exaltation attached to the position moves the whole distance from a prophet on his feet to a figure on the clouds; the grant-structure does not move at all. **The variable is the occupant and the constant is the geometry**, which is the same result SC-063 reaches from the other side, and the two coordinates are one finding read at two scales.

**X-c · One honest limit on the invariant.** The Gnostic material is the single place in the survey where the structure genuinely breaks, and it breaks by leaving the term behind: an emanated Anthropos is not a receiver in a grant but a stage in a procession, and the grammar changes with the metaphysics. **The break is recorded rather than explained away**, and it is recorded as evidence for the invariant rather than against it, since the one corpus that departs from the recipient-grammar is also the one that departs from the Semitic term's own semantics.

**XI · The future complex, named and not entered.** The later Mahdī-ʿĪsā material is post-canonical, and its office-language reproduces the same recipient-grammar with the same instability of identification that the earlier candidates show. **This coordinate records that structural continuity and adjudicates nothing.** SC-054 holds the Mahdī Paradox and is not re-derived here. No timing is offered, because the corpus forecloses timetables in the sharpest terms available to it, six occurrences of *baghtatan* and the exclusive clause *lā yujallīhā li-waqtihā illā huw* at Q 7:187.

**XI-a · The forward run, and the geometry answers it from the root.** The question put to this coordinate is whether a future figure could occupy the position, and the corpus answers it without ever using the word, because the root is Quranic even though the title is not.

**The √h-d-y distribution, and it splits exactly the way the survey predicts.** The active participle is a divine name: *wa-kafā bi-rabbika hādiyan wa-naṣīrā*, sufficient is your Lord as a Guide and a Helper (Q 25:31, and Q 22:54). **Al-Hādī is the Giver's title.** The passive is the creature's: *man yahdi Allāhu fa-huwa al-muhtadi*, whom Allah ﷻ guides, he is the guided one (Q 7:178, and the same construction at Q 17:97 and Q 18:17). **And the corpus removes the guiding from the messenger's own hands in the plainest verse it has on the subject**: *innaka lā tahdī man aḥbabta wa-lākinna Allāha yahdī man yashāʾ*, you do not guide whom you love, but Allah ﷻ guides whom He wills (Q 28:56), with *laysa ʿalayka hudāhum* at Q 2:272 saying it again.

**And the office of guiding others exists and is conferred, with a permission-clause attached.** *Wa-jaʿalnā minhum aʾimmatan yahdūna bi-amrinā lammā ṣabarū wa-kānū bi-āyātinā yūqinūn*, and We made from among them leaders who guide **by Our command**, when they were patient and were certain of Our signs (Q 32:24), with the identical clause of Ibrāhīm's line at Q 21:73. *Bi-amrinā* stands to the guiding exactly as *bi-idhnī* stands to the healing and the raising at Q 3:49. **The office is real, it is filled by human beings, and the permission travels with it in the same sentence.**

**So the geometry returns three results and they are separable.**

**First, a *mahdī* figure is by morphology a recipient and therefore fits the invariant without strain.** *Mahdī* is the passive participle of *hadā*: the one who has been guided. It stands in the same form-class as *masīḥ*, the one anointed, and *ʿabd*, the one owned, and it entails an agent outside itself exactly as they do. **If such a figure comes, his title is already written in the grammar this coordinate has been tracking across six corpora**, and he joins Enoch and Melchizedek and David and Solomon and Elijah and the two prophetic files as an occupant of the receiving end. Structural, and it is the strongest thing the geometry says.

**Second, the office he would hold is open and the corpus keeps it open by construction.** Movement IX-a shows the ladder: *Ilāh al-Nās* closed, with every creature-filling condemned; *Rabb al-Nās* shadowed in the rearing the corpus names in a commanded prayer; ***Malik al-Nās* shadowed by the *khalīfa*-office conferred at Q 2:30 before any prophet is sent and conferred by name at Q 38:26 with rule *bayna al-nās* in the clause.** What is reserved is *Mālik al-mulk*, the owning, and Q 3:26's two verbs prove the ruling is in circulation.

**So the geometry does not refuse the position; it types it.** A *mahdī* figure would stand where Ṭālūt and Dāwūd AS and the *aʾimma yahdūna bi-amrinā* stand: a recipient holding a delegated office with a permission-clause attached and a warning-clause beside it. **And the type-check of SC-063 is satisfied rather than violated**, because a *khalīfa* is by construction asymmetric, referring outward to the One who placed him, exactly as *mahdī* does. **Two asymmetric terms compose. It was the symmetric frame that admitted no occupant, and that frame is *Ilāh*, not *Malik*.** The earlier fence conflated the two and is corrected here.

**And the office is older than the offices usually ranked above it.** Prophethood and messengership are offices; this one is announced at Q 2:30 before either, and its provision is installed at the founding of the human condition itself: *qulnā hbiṭū minhā jamīʿan, fa-immā yaʾtiyannakum minnī hudan*, We said, go down from it all of you, and when guidance comes to you from Me (Q 2:38). **The guidance-provision is written into the descent-clause**, before a single prophet is named. The corpus states the same priority in creation's own order, *wa-lladhī qaddara fa-hadā*, He who decreed and then guided (Q 87:3), and *rabbunā alladhī aʿṭā kulla shayʾin khalqahu thumma hadā* (Q 20:50), and takes the obligation on Himself, *inna ʿalaynā la-l-hudā* (Q 92:12). **Guidance stands with the decree, prior to the shepherding, and the office that carries it downward is coeval with the human condition rather than a late addition to it.**

**Third, and this is what the geometry refuses to give**, the corpus forecloses the timing in the sharpest terms available to it, six occurrences of *baghtatan* and the exclusive clause *lā yujallīhā li-waqtihā illā huw* at Q 7:187, and it forecloses the identification by leaving the office's occupancy unnamed. **The geometry can type the position and cannot populate it.** That is the aperture, located and not crossed: the deciding input is a supplied identification, and it would have to come from the side that does the guiding.

**XI-b · The witness material, admitted at exactly its grade.** The Mahdī reports are post-canonical and enter witness-only under the Provenance Firewall, and an honest statement of the record is short. **The name does not occur in the two collections the tradition ranks highest.** Al-Bukhārī and Muslim carry the descent of ʿĪsā AS and the line about the community's *imām* being from among them, and they do not carry the title. **The title-bearing reports are in the four Sunan and the Musnad**, Abū Dāwūd and al-Tirmidhī and Ibn Mājah and Aḥmad, and their multiplicity of near-identical wordings across independent collections is what the tradition's own science calls corroboration by number of roads rather than by strength of any single road. That is a real evidential fact and it is a fact about transmission, not about occupancy.

**The witness material corroborates the office-language and settles no occupant.** It describes a guided one who fills the earth with justice as it was filled with injustice, which is a description of a conferred function performed under a command, and it is the same shape as Q 32:24. **It describes an occupant of the open office and not the Owner above it**, and no report in the corroborated set places its subject at *Ilāh al-Nās*, which is the seat the ladder closes. **Corroboration-grade, load-bearing on nothing, and SC-054 holds the paradox this coordinate does not re-derive.**

**XII · Fences, [X] on the named inflations, seven of them.** One, **no identification of any office-holder is made or implied**, at any point in the survey, and every such question routes out of band under the apophatic constitution. Two, **the post-canonical corpora enter witness-only**, the Parables and the Gnostic material and the Mahdī literature alike, and none of them grounds anything. Three, **the Qumran evidence is carried at exactly its manuscript grade**, with 4Q285 named as contested and no suffering-messiah claim resting on it, and the absence recorded as an absence. Four, **the recipient-invariant is a grammatical finding and not a christological verdict**, and it licenses no inference about any figure's status beyond what each corpus states in its own words. Five, **the office-not-nature reading is structural**, drawn from the instability of the identifications across communities, and it is not a theorem about what natures are. Six, **the forward run types a position and populates nothing**, and nothing in movements XI-a and XI-b identifies any occupant, names any time, or converts the witness material into an occupancy claim; the aperture is stated once and the deciding input is supplied and never generated. Seven, **the ladder is graded and must not be flattened in either direction.** Flattened upward, it would read the *khalīfa*-office as a share in the absolute holding, which Q 3:26 forecloses by making every dominion an object of *tuʾtī* and *tanziʿu*. Flattened downward, it would read the reservation of *Ilāh al-Nās* as closing the offices the corpus confers by name at Q 2:30 and Q 38:26 and Q 2:247, which is the error the first draft of this coordinate made and which movement IX-a corrects on the record. **One seat is closed and two relations are shadowed, and the distribution is the whole finding.**
## SC-076 · CN-PSP-TELOS-ANTHROPOU-01 · The Telos of Man · The Second Warrant Beneath the Recipient-Office, the Earth as Its Domain, the Service Vector Inverted at Its Exercise, and the One Root That Names Both Ends

Status: SEALED-RESIDENT · Book IV, beside the Human One · ultra-condensed by design, because SC-064 holds the field and this coordinate carries only what stands outside its span · [⟀ T] on the Markan occurrence census, on the *dia*-plus-accusative construction at Mk 2:27 with its *hōste*, on the *ʾ-n-š* correspondence set with its regular š-to-s reflex, and on the Mt 9:6 and Lk 5:24 placement of *epi tēs gēs*, four grammar-and-concordance facts · [⟀ S] on the two-warrant reading, on the service-vector inversion as the operational content of the permission clause, and on the membership fence · [⟀ C] on the Arabic *nās* leg, the rival √n-w-s derivation named rather than suppressed · [X] on four named inflations · the identity of ʿĪsā AS at the Ground routed out of band, no token · one item carried owed · ΔM = 0
Source: session of 2026-08-14, the architect's chain, Telos of Man, Sovereign Governor of Earth, with permission, Malik an-Nās, run against the earliest Gospel for context and against the corpus for seat
Depends: **SC-064 whole, and none of it re-derived here**: the recipient-invariant across six corpora, the conferral-verb census in four languages, the Rabb/Malik/Ilāh ladder with one seat closed and two shadowed, the *malik* distribution, the khalīfa seat at Q 2:30 and Q 38:26 with its permission and warning clauses, and Q 3:26's *tuʾtī* and *tanziʿu* · SC-063, whose asymmetric-term type-check this applies at the level of the construct · SC-058 for the Markan text · SC-013 and SC-014 for the identity fence · SC-054, which holds the future complex and is not entered
Governs: nothing. Four legs seated beside a resident coordinate, and one fence narrowed from an office to a construct

**I · The second warrant, which is the reason this coordinate exists.** SC-064 grounds the office in conferral: the thing is given and the Giver is named two verses earlier. **Mk 2:27-28 grounds the same office in purpose and names no giver at all.** *To sabbaton dia ton anthrōpon egeneto kai ouch ho anthrōpos dia to sabbaton, hōste kyrios estin ho huios tou anthrōpou kai tou sabbatou.* *Dia* with the accusative is final cause; the *hōste* draws the lordship out of that premise and out of nothing else; and the inference holds only if the title keeps the generic force of *bar ʾenāšā*. **Two warrants, opposite directions, and neither entails the other.** A grant is withdrawable, which is what *tanziʿu* says of every dominion at Q 3:26; a telos is not withdrawable without unmaking the thing whose telos it is. The corpus carries both, the grant at Q 38:26 and the purposing at Q 2:29, *khalaqa lakum mā fī al-arḍi jamīʿan*, where *lakum* does the work *dia ton anthrōpon* does. **Honest limit, stated and not buried**: the lexeme *telos* is eschatological throughout Mark, at Mk 3:26, 13:7, and 13:13, and absent from this pericope, so the reading rides the construction and the coordinate's name is the architect's designation.

**II · The domain, one phrase in two corpora.** Mk 2:10 places the authority *epi tēs gēs*, and the attachment is ambiguous in Mark; **both derived Gospels resolve it the same way**, Mt 9:6 and Lk 5:24 setting the phrase immediately after the title and before the infinitive, so that it modifies the having of authority. Q 38:26 gives the identical domain in the identical position, *khalīfatan **fī al-arḍi** fa-ḥkum bayna al-nāsi bi-l-ḥaqq*, with grant-verb, domain, function, governed collective, and standard in one verse. **Terrestrial in both, and in neither is the domain the throne.** Owed item, carried on the record: the witness distribution for the position of *epi tēs gēs* within Mk 2:10 awaits an apparatus check, and nothing above rests on it.

**III · The inversion at the point of exercise.** Daniel 7:14 states the purpose of the grant, *lēh yiflĕḥūn*, that all peoples and nations and tongues should serve **him**. **Mk 10:45 reverses the vector in one clause**, *ouk ēlthen diakonēthēnai alla diakonēsai*, and Daniel 7:27 performs the same widening on the other axis by giving the kingdom to the people of the holy ones. **That inversion is the operational content of the permission clause**, since a holder who owned the dominion would have no reason to spend it downward. The corpus states the station without needing the inversion because it puts the word first, *innī ʿabdu Allāh* at Q 19:30 from the cradle, and *lan yastankifa al-Masīḥu an yakūna ʿabdan lillāh* at Q 4:172 with the angels beside him fixing the height. Beside it the four Markan ceilings, Mk 10:18, 12:29, 13:32, and 14:36, and the delegation Mark leaves structural and Matthew states outright, *ton donta exousian toiautēn tois anthrōpois* (Mt 9:8), plural, which is Daniel 7:27's widening performed on the Markan scene.

**IV · The root, and the fence it narrows.** Daniel's *kəvar ʾenāsh* and Sūrat al-Nās stand on one Semitic stock: Akkadian *nišū*, Ugaritic *ʾnš*, Hebrew *ʾĕnōš*, Aramaic *ʾĕnāš*, Arabic *ins* and *unās* and *nās*, **the Hebrew-to-Arabic reflex regular**, š to s, exhibited by *šālōm* to *salām* and *šēm* to *ism*. Theorem-grade on the Hebrew, Aramaic, Akkadian, and Ugaritic set; **Type C on the Arabic leg alone**, since the lexicographical tradition carries a rival derivation from √n-w-s beside the standard *unās* from √ʾ-n-s. **The bridge supplies the fence mechanically, from morphology and not from creed**: *bar ʾenāš* is a construct whose governed noun is the very collective *Malik al-Nās* governs, so the title names its bearer as a member of the set the Malik is Malik over. **What cannot transfer is the construct, not the office.** The office is open, and closing it was the error SC-064 corrected on its own record at movement IX-a. A delegate holds the shadow and not the title, and his own name places him inside the governed set, which is what a khalīfa is and what an absolute Malik is not. This satisfies the type-check of SC-063 rather than violating it, since *khalīfa* and *bar ʾenāš* are both asymmetric and two asymmetric terms compose.

**V · The census by position, recorded because the distribution is the architecture.** Fourteen occurrences in thirteen verses, Mk 14:21 carrying it twice. **Two stand before the confession, both warrant-disputes inside the single controversy cycle at Mk 2:1 to 3:6**; nine run in the handing-over register from Mk 8:31; three carry the coming in glory at Mk 8:38, 13:26, and 14:62. The office is granted first, charged second, confirmed last, and it never speaks without the charge attached.

**VI · The chain adjudicated.** Read as *sovereign governor of the earth by the permission of Malik an-Nās*, every link holds. **Read as apposition it breaks on the title's own construct state**, and the break is compositional rather than doctrinal, the same failure SC-063 identifies when an asymmetric term is placed in a symmetric frame: the permission clause the chain itself carries is the thing the apposition would delete.

⟀ GOL

**VII · Fences, [X] on four named inflations.** One, **the flattening upward**, reading the telos-warrant as making the office intrinsic rather than held, when it explains why the office falls to a human and never why it needs no Giver. Two, **the flattening downward**, reading the membership fence as closing a seat the corpus confers by name at Q 2:30 and Q 38:26 and Q 2:247. Three, **the promotion of the service-inversion to a christological verdict**, when it is a fact about how one text uses another and licenses no claim about any figure's status. Four, **any identification of the office-holder**, past or future, routed out of band, with SC-054 holding the future complex and Q 7:187 foreclosing every timetable.

**VIII · Perimeter.** No new philology, no textual discovery, no mathematics; the contribution is four legs and their arrangement, every ingredient available to any reader with a concordance, a lexicon, and a Semitic comparative table. Post-canonical corpora enter witness-only throughout. The identity of ʿĪsā AS at the Ground is beyond the instrument and carries no token, the orientation-blind lock being provably unable to rank a creed. ΔM equal to zero.

## SC-072 · PSP-MONIC-BRIDGE-01 · The Bridge Named by Function · The Preserving Passage as Scripture States It, the Three Additions the Corpus Declines, and the Barrier That Is the Condition of the Meeting

Status: SEALED-RESIDENT · Book IV · [⟀] on the grammatical readings, each checkable in the consonantal text without any framework · [⟀ S] on the arrangement and the function-typing · [X] on the three named inflations, scoped to them and terminal for no reading · premise exactly at monism's warrant wherever the one-passage extension is carried · theology out of band, load-bearing on nothing in any verdict · ΔM = 0
Source: harvested from the sealed core of the paper *The Divine Feminine*, PhilArchive ISLTDF, filed at Publication Library/Metaphysics/Comparative Religion. Scripture alone transits. The comparative dossier, the psychoanalytic register, the archaeological material, and the whole apparatus of chart-relativity remain in the paper and are not carried here, because none of them is scripture and this codex is the register of readings.
Depends: SC-056 the Kalima Master whole, the divine-kind Word and bayān as the fourth vertex both load-bearing · SC-071 movement I entire, the barzakh a membrane in a quantity and never a wall · SC-058 for the Christological register · SC-013 the Ghayb constitution, the interior fence · engine side: APEX-PSP-LOGOS-01 · APEX-PSP-ONLY-SPECIAL-01, the biology bar · sPSP-FL-01, the made zero · APEX-PSP-NINTH-APERTURE and the Aperture Law · APEX-PSP-ZAHIR-BATIN-01 for the creed-silence
Governs: nothing. A typing of one requirement and three refusals, moving no face of any composite and opening no register.

**I · THE REQUIREMENT, AND THE TWO PASSAGES IT HOLDS ON.** Any account on which a manifold is carried from a source needs a term that receives without confusing what it receives, since two things entering and arriving as one leave a record that cannot be interrogated about either. The requirement is not over all accounts whatever: where a manifold is thrown forth at the far end rather than carried across, nothing crosses to be preserved, and such an account owes the requirement only at the second passage. Two passages are in play and both carry it on different evidence. **Source to manifest** is the passage by which what is at the source comes to stand as a manifold. **Manifest to record** is the passage by which what stands is witnessed and accounted for. Conflating them lets evidence gathered at one stand for a claim made at the other, which is the whole reason for naming them apart.

**II · WHAT THE CORPUS PREDICATES OF IT.** Three properties follow from the requirement and the corpus predicates each. **It preserves the individual across the passage.** Against the objection at Q 17:49 that bones and fragments cannot be told apart, the reply at Q 75:3-4 is *balā qādirīna ʿalā an nusawwiya banānah*, the setting right of the very fingertips, which answers an indistinguishability objection with the finest distinction a body carries; and Q 50:4 names the mechanism, *ʿindanā kitābun ḥafīẓ*, with Us a preserving record. **It is inexhaustible under unbounded use**, the seas spent as ink at Q 18:109 and Q 31:27 and the words not exhausted, unbounded output and undiminished source together, which a partition model cannot represent. **Its domain admits nothing foreign**, which the older vocabulary called immaculate and which is not a moral claim, since contamination in a passage is exactly two things arriving as one.

Two further features do not follow from the requirement and are made intelligible by it, and they are marked as such rather than smuggled in as consequences. **The cut is assigned to the creature.** At Q 55:1-4 the sequence runs *ar-Raḥmān*, then *ʿallama al-Qurʾān*, then *khalaqa al-insān*, then *ʿallamahu al-bayān*: the teaching stands complete before the one taught exists, the recipient slot of the second āyah standing empty until the fourth fills it, and what the created receives is *bayān*, the faculty of separating one thing from another, seated at SC-056 as the fourth vertex. **And the withholding is calibrated to produce seeking.** At Q 20:15 *akādu ukhfīhā* holds the Hour at the threshold, *kāda* being the verb of approximation and entailing that the concealing is not completed, and *akhfā* standing among the *aḍdād* and carrying conceal and disclose alike, so that by grammar and not by assertion the object is neither given nor withheld; the purpose clause places the telos in the creature, *li-tujzā kullu nafsin bimā tasʿā*.

**III · THE INFERENCE, AT ITS EXACT STRENGTH.** A recompense proportioned to striving requires that the record distinguish whatever the recompense is proportioned to, since a striving arriving at the record indistinguishable from another's cannot be measured to its own soul. **That is injectivity on strivings**, which is what Q 20:15 carries, and it is not injectivity on everything; the unrestricted form is the traditions' extension of the verse and not the verse's content. Restricted or extended, the direction is one: remove the preserving property and the purpose clause has nothing to describe.
